  - **Verified against real Postgres** (ephemeral Docker instance + `alembic upgrade head` + `scripts/seed_db.py`; container removed after), both entry points: (1) rank mode — `submit_rating(..., accept_action=PARTIAL_ACCEPT, accept_accepted_context_ids=[kept_id])` called with **no** `accept_edited_answer` argument succeeded; the resulting `HitlDecision.edited_answer_enc` was `NULL`; the kept `patient_context` row correctly became `reviewer_edited` and the not-kept row was correctly expired — the accepted-context split alone drove the real effect, exactly as `_apply_patient_context_effect` promises. (2) escalation resolution — `apply_decision(..., action=PARTIAL_ACCEPT, reason_code=...)` called with **no** `edited_answer` and **no** `span_actions` succeeded; `HitlDecision.edited_answer_enc` was `NULL`; the escalation correctly resolved to `state=resolved, resolution=partial`. Offline suite: **481 passed** (was 479) — `test_submit_rating_reject_requires_reason_code`'s sibling `test_submit_rating_partial_accept_requires_edited_answer` (HTTP-layer) replaced with `test_submit_rating_partial_accept_succeeds_without_edited_answer` (asserts `200`, not `422`); `test_rubric_workflow.py` gained `test_submit_rating_partial_accept_succeeds_without_edited_answer` (asserts `edited_answer_enc is None`) alongside the existing volunteered-text test (now asserting `is not None`, confirming both paths work); `test_hitl_routes.py` gained `test_partial_accept_succeeds_without_edited_answer`. `make lint`/`make typecheck` clean (typecheck at the same pre-existing 10-finding baseline). Frontend: `npm run build`/`npm run lint --max-warnings 0` both clean.
- **Reversible?:** yes — removing a required-field check only loosens both contracts; nothing in this codebase relied on `edited_answer`/`accept_edited_answer` being non-null (the encryption call was already conditional on it), so no downstream code needed to change, only the two schema validators and the one shared frontend validity check.

### 102. Post-approval `docker compose up` smoke test (dev profile) — proxy healthcheck was silently, consistently broken (IPv4/IPv6 resolver mismatch)
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-15 / post-Phase-5 (operator-requested verification, ARCH-036 / PRD-106)
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-036, PRD-106
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** none — a real infrastructure bug, found by actually running the full `dev`-profile stack (not re-deriving from the DEVIATIONS #95 pass, which predates every Phase 5 change) and checking each container's own reported health, not just that requests succeeded through it.
- **What was verified working, end to end, against a genuinely fresh deployment** (`docker compose --profile dev down -v` then `up -d` — a clean `pg-data`/`qdrant-data`/`redis-data`/`hf-model-cache` volume set, not the carried-over state from earlier sessions): `alembic upgrade head` to `e6bd710e1911`; `scripts/seed_db.py` (11 rubric domains, 4 roles, 5 demo users, 2 field-policy rows); real PDF ingestion (`POST /ingest/documents` → `worker`'s `process_document` → real parse/chunk/embed/Qdrant upsert, confirmed via worker logs and a direct `SELECT` against `corpus.chunk`); `POST /auth/dev-login` for all three roles; a `scope_1`-shaped query correctly returning `no_guideline` (expected under `EMBEDDING_BACKEND=stub`/`llm-gateway`'s stub server — `app.llm.stub.stub_embed` is an explicitly-documented "deterministic, not meaningful" hash-based pseudo-embedding and `stub_chat` always returns a fixed non-answer by design, so no real grounded synthesis is possible in this profile; this is the safety fallback working correctly, not a defect); a `scope_2_excluded` query correctly escalating with **zero** recommendation content; the full escalation lifecycle (`GET /hitl/escalations` list → `GET .../{id}` triggering `open → in_review` → `POST .../decision`); a real combined rank+accept `POST /rubric/results/{id}/ratings` call (against a directly-inserted `eval.result` row, since no live writer populates the review queue yet — DEVIATIONS #79) with `accept_action=reject` and **no** reason code, confirming #100/#101's relaxed requirements hold through the actual running container, not just the offline suite/ad hoc verification scripts used while building them; `GET /admin/audit?verify=true` showing a fully intact hash chain (`broken_chain_ids: []`) on the fresh volume; the frontend SPA served through the proxy with the correct `<title>`; and the auth boundary (401 no token, 403 wrong role) through the real proxy path a browser uses.
- **What was broken:** the `proxy` service's own Docker `HEALTHCHECK` (`docker-compose.yml`) reported `unhealthy` with `FailingStreak: 10` — consistently, not intermittently — despite nginx correctly serving every real request throughout (confirmed via `curl` against both `:80` and `:443` from the host throughout the same window the healthcheck was failing). Root cause, confirmed by exec'ing into the container: nginx's `listen 80;`/`listen 443 ssl;` (`deploy/nginx/nginx.conf`) bind IPv4 only (`netstat` showed `0.0.0.0:80`/`0.0.0.0:443`, never `:::80`/`:::443`); the container's `/etc/hosts` resolves `localhost` to **both** `127.0.0.1` and `::1`; the healthcheck's `wget -qO- http://localhost/healthz` (busybox wget, the `nginx:1.27-alpine` image's wget) resolved `::1` and got `Connection refused` — no IPv6 listener exists — with no fallback to the IPv4 address (confirmed directly: `wget http://127.0.0.1/healthz` inside the same container succeeded immediately; `wget http://[::1]/healthz` reproduced the exact failure). `api`'s own healthcheck (`python -c "urllib.request.urlopen('http://localhost:8000/healthz')"`) does **not** hit this — Python's resolver/connection logic falls back across the address family list where busybox wget does not — confirmed by checking `api`'s container also has the same dual-stack `/etc/hosts` entry, yet reports `healthy` correctly.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **Fixed by pointing the healthcheck at `127.0.0.1` explicitly, not `localhost`** — a one-line change (`docker-compose.yml`'s `proxy.healthcheck.test`), not a change to nginx's own listen directives. Making nginx also bind `[::]:80`/`[::]:443` (dual-stack) was considered and rejected as the wrong scope for this fix: the real requirement (ARCH-036/PRD-106, self-hosted deployment) never asks for IPv6 support, only that the healthcheck accurately reflect whether the service can serve real requests — and it already can, over the IPv4 the compose network and the host port-publish both actually use. Pointing the check at the address that's actually listening is the narrower, lower-risk fix.
  - **Why this matters even though nothing in `docker-compose.yml` gates on `proxy`'s health** (its own `depends_on: [api, frontend]` has no `condition: service_healthy`, so the false-unhealthy status never blocked startup or broke the compose graph): a misleading `unhealthy` status on the one container an operator/monitoring system is most likely to check first (it's the one with published ports) is a real operational hazard on a real server — it would misdirect debugging effort, and any external orchestration layered on top of Docker's own health status (a load balancer, an uptime check, a future Swarm/Kubernetes migration modeling its liveness probe on this compose file) would incorrectly treat a fully working proxy as down.
  - **Re-verified after the fix:** `docker compose up -d proxy` (recreate only, the other 7 services untouched) → `docker inspect .../State.Health.Status` → `healthy` within one check interval; re-ran the host-side smoke checks (`/healthz`, `/`) immediately after — both still `200`, confirming the fix didn't change nginx's actual serving behavior, only what the healthcheck measures. Full backend suite (`make lint`, `make test` — 481 passed, unaffected by a compose-file-only change) re-run to confirm no regression from touching `docker-compose.yml`.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a one-line healthcheck target change; no effect on nginx's config, routing, TLS, or any request path.

### 103. Wiring a real self-hosted gateway (chat + embeddings) end to end — a wrong wire-contract assumption corrected, `EMBEDDING_BACKEND=gateway` implemented, TLS/SNI support added, a real UID permission bug found and fixed
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-15 / post-Phase-5 (operator-requested verification, ARCH-004/005, PRD-101/103)
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-004, ARCH-005, ARCH-012, PRD-101, PRD-103
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** the operator, testing the dockerized stack, hit "no guideline found" on every query — expected under the `dev` profile's stub embeddings/chat (DEVIATIONS #98/#102's own caveat) — and then supplied real credentials for the same operator-run gateway DEVIATIONS #42 had already scaffolded config for but explicitly deferred implementing, to see a real answer. Two judgment calls came up while wiring it in, both put to the operator directly rather than resolved unilaterally: (1) `LLMGateway.chat()` and its whole test suite were built and tested against an assumed OpenAI-style response (`choices: [{"message": {"content"}}]`), documented as "the OpenAI-ish gateway contract" — but the real gateway returns a flatter, different shape (`{"content", "finish_reason", "usage"}`), and `/v1/embeddings` similarly returns `{"embeddings": [[...]]}`, not OpenAI's `data: [{"embedding"}]`. This assumption had never actually been checked against the real gateway for chat (DEVIATIONS #42 verified only the embeddings shape, and even that one was recorded in a comment but never implemented). (2) which gateway integration scope the operator wanted (retrieval-only vs. full chat+retrieval; their own gateway vs. me standing up a local model server).
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **(1) — operator chose "fix the contract to match the real gateway."** `LLMGateway.chat()`'s parsing changed from `data["choices"][0]["message"]["content"]` to `data["content"]`; `app/ingestion/embed.py`'s new `gateway` branch parses `data["embeddings"]`, not `data["data"][*]["embedding"]`; `app/llm/stub_server.py` (the dev/CI stub) was updated to emit the same real shapes, so the offline stub actually imitates the real target gateway instead of an unverified assumption. `tests/test_llm_gateway.py`'s module docstring and every mocked response payload updated to match; a naive lexical gating test (`test_no_model_name_literals_in_gateway_source`, constraint #6's "no hardcoded model name" check) false-positived on the word "Ollama" in a docstring (its `"llama-"` substring check doesn't know "Ollama" isn't a banned model-name literal) — fixed by rewording, not by weakening the test.
  - **(2) — operator chose the full scope**: both chat and embeddings against their real gateway, using their own already-running instance (not a locally-stood-up model server).
  - **`EMBEDDING_BACKEND=gateway` implemented** (`app/ingestion/embed.py`, DEVIATIONS #42's scaffolding finally consumed): `POST {embedding_gateway_url}/v1/embeddings`, Bearer auth, `embedding_model_id` as the gateway's own tag (`qllama/bge-large-en-v1.5:latest`, confirmed live). Client construction/caching mirrors the existing `_get_local_model` lru_cache pattern (`_get_gateway_client`), with the same test-injection seam (`_GATEWAY_CLIENT_BUILDER`, monkeypatchable to an `httpx.MockTransport`-backed client — no real network call in the offline suite).
  - **Real model ids verified live, not guessed** (constraint #5): `MODEL_ID=qwen3.5:9b` confirmed via the gateway's own `GET /health` (`{"model": "qwen3.5:9b"}`); `EMBEDDING_MODEL_ID=qllama/bge-large-en-v1.5:latest` confirmed via a real successful `POST /v1/embeddings` call (1024-dim vectors returned) and cross-checked against the gateway's own `/metrics` (`gateway_backend_health{backend_id="ollama-primary",model="qllama/bge-large-en-v1.5:latest"} 1.0`). Both `*_VERIFIED=true` in `.env` reflect this, not an assumption.
  - **TLS: self-signed cert trusted, never disabled** (consistent with DEVIATIONS #54's "never turn verification off" stance). The gateway's cert (`CN=localhost`, self-signed, SANs `localhost`/`127.0.0.1`/`0.0.0.0`) is extracted once (`openssl s_client` + `x509`) to `deploy/tls/qwen_gateway_ca.pem` (already gitignored via the existing `deploy/tls/*.pem` rule — never committed) and mounted read-only into `api`/`worker` (`docker-compose.yml`, `./deploy/tls:/app/deploy/tls:ro`). New `Settings.llm_gateway_ca_bundle`/`embedding_gateway_ca_bundle` (paths) are passed straight to `httpx.Client(verify=...)` — `verify` accepts a CA-bundle path natively; full chain verification, never `verify=False`.
  - **A second, narrower TLS problem needed a second fix: hostname verification.** The container reaches the gateway (published on the HOST's own `localhost:8443`) via `host.docker.internal` (`docker-compose.yml` `extra_hosts: ["host.docker.internal:host-gateway"]`, the standard Linux-Engine equivalent of Docker Desktop's built-in name) — but the cert's SANs don't include `host.docker.internal`, so hostname verification correctly failed (`Hostname mismatch, certificate is not valid for 'host.docker.internal'`) even with the right CA trusted. The cert's IP SAN (`127.0.0.1`) can't be used either — a container's own `127.0.0.1` is its own loopback, never the host's, by Linux network-namespace design; `network_mode: host` would fix that but was rejected as far too invasive (it would pull `api`/`worker` off the compose bridge network entirely, breaking service-name DNS to `postgres`/`redis`/`qdrant`). **Actual fix:** new `Settings.llm_gateway_sni_hostname`/`embedding_gateway_sni_hostname`, passed as httpx's per-request `extensions={"sni_hostname": "localhost"}` — this overrides only the TLS SNI sent and the hostname checked against the cert, not which address is actually connected to (`llm_gateway_url` itself, still `host.docker.internal`) and not whether verification runs at all. Confirmed empirically before wiring it in (a one-off script against the exact `httpx.Client(verify=<path>)` construction `LLMGateway`/`embed.py` actually use) that this keeps full chain + hostname verification passing, just against the correct name. Covered by new tests (`test_chat_sends_sni_hostname_extension_when_set`/`test_chat_omits_..._by_default` in `tests/test_llm_gateway.py`; the gateway-embedding equivalent in `tests/test_embed.py`) asserting the extension reaches the request via `httpx.MockTransport`, not just that it doesn't crash.
  - **A real bug found on the first live query attempt: `hf-model-cache` named-volume permission mismatch.** Switching `RERANKER_BACKEND` to `local` (the gateway has no rerank endpoint at all — confirmed via its own `openapi.json` paths list, matching DEVIATIONS #44's decision not to assume gateway reranking) triggered a real `sentence-transformers`/HuggingFace download on first use, which failed: `Permission denied: '/home/appuser/.cache/huggingface/hub'`. Root cause: `backend/Dockerfile` creates non-root `appuser` (uid 10001) and `chown -R`s `/app`, but never creates `/home/appuser/.cache/huggingface` at build time — Docker initializes a *fresh* named volume's content from whatever's at that path in the image layer, and since nothing existed there, it defaulted to `root:root`, unwritable by uid 10001. The exact same class of bug as DEVIATIONS #95 finding #3 (a `data/` *bind* mount's host/container UID mismatch), here hitting a *named volume* instead. **Fix:** `Dockerfile` now `mkdir -p`s the cache dir and `chown`s it alongside `/app`, so a freshly-created `hf-model-cache` volume inherits `appuser` ownership from the image layer. Verified: `docker compose down -v` (forcing volume recreation) + rebuild + `ls -la /home/appuser/.cache/` inside the container showed `appuser:appuser`, and the reranker model then downloaded and loaded successfully.
  - **Also fixed while wiring this in:** `.env`'s pre-existing `EMBEDDING_GATEWAY_URL` already included the endpoint path (`https://localhost:8443/v1/embeddings`) — since `app.ingestion.embed`'s new gateway client appends `/v1/embeddings` itself (`httpx.Client(base_url=...)` + `client.post("/v1/embeddings", ...)`, matching `LLMGateway`'s own pattern), the old value would have double-appended the path the moment the branch was ever implemented. Caught before it ever ran, not after a failure — corrected to a base URL only, with a comment explaining why.
  - **End-to-end result against the real stack** (fresh `docker compose --profile dev down -v && up -d`, migrate, seed, real PDF ingestion): worker logs confirmed real `POST https://host.docker.internal:8443/v1/embeddings` calls succeeding during ingestion (1024-dim vectors, matching the gateway's real model); a real `/query` call produced retrieval that actually found the right guideline section (`scope_1`, not `no_guideline` this time) and a real, substantively accurate synthesized candidate answer from `qwen3.5:9b` — but the deterministic citation-verifier flagged all 8 of its segments `quote_mismatch` (the model paraphrased rather than reproducing verbatim quotes, despite `app/agents/prompts/guideline_synthesis.md` explicitly instructing "a **verbatim quote** copied from that passage") and correctly escalated (`trigger_code: grounding_failure`, zero segments released) rather than showing an unverifiable answer — the grounding gate (PRD-NFR-2, ARCH's citation-integrity requirement) working exactly as designed, end to end, against a real model for the first time this project has run one. Getting this specific model to reliably emit properly quote-anchored segments is a prompt-tuning/model-capability question, explicitly out of scope for this entry — flagged to the operator as a distinct follow-up, not silently declared "done."
- **Reversible?:** yes throughout. The wire-contract fix is the one change with a real blast radius (anyone who'd built code against the old, wrong, never-actually-correct `choices`-shaped assumption would need to update it) — but nothing in this codebase had; `EMBEDDING_BACKEND=gateway`/TLS settings/SNI override are all new, additive, empty-by-default config surfaces; the Dockerfile change only adds a directory + chown, no removed capability; `.env`'s corrected `EMBEDDING_GATEWAY_URL` fixes a bug that had not yet shipped a single successful call under the old value.

### 104. Root cause of the real `quote_mismatch` escalation: PDF line-wrap newlines, not the model — whitespace-tolerant verbatim matching, verified with a real released, cited answer
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-15 / post-Phase-5 (operator-requested follow-up to DEVIATIONS #103)
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-014, ARCH-015, PRD-011, PRD-016, PRD-NFR-2 (citation/grounding integrity)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** DEVIATIONS #103 ended with a real query against the real gateway producing a real, accurate candidate answer that the citation-verifier correctly rejected as `quote_mismatch` on every segment, flagged as an open question — "prompt-tuning / model-capability, explicitly out of scope." The operator asked to pursue it ("try to get a released, cited answer"). Before touching the synthesis prompt (which explicitly already asks for verbatim quotes) or considering it a model-capability limitation, the actual cause needed diagnosing, not assumed.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **Root cause, found by comparing the real retrieved chunk's raw stored text (`SELECT text FROM corpus.chunk ...`, inspected with `cat -A` to reveal literal whitespace) against the model's candidate answer:** the guideline PDF's text extraction preserves the source PDF's line-wrap points as literal `\n` characters mid-sentence (e.g. `"...should be\nstimulated by rubbing the back 2–3 times..."`) — a layout artifact of the original PDF, not a semantic paragraph/list break. The model, asked to quote verbatim, reproduced the words in order with ordinary single spaces (exactly what any reasonable reading of "verbatim quote" should mean) — but the grounding gate's quote check was a byte-exact Python `in`/`.find()` substring test, so a newline standing in for a space silently failed every single claim, regardless of model behavior. This was **not** a prompt-adherence or model-capability problem — the model was already doing the right thing.
  - **Four call sites, one shared fix, not four separate patches.** The byte-exact check existed independently in `app.grounding.verifier._verify_claim_segment` (the boolean quote_mismatch verdict), `app.grounding.verifier.citations_for_segments` (a second, separate guard before calling `build_citation`), `app.citations.model.build_citation` (`text.find(quote)`, computing the real offsets), and `app.citations.model.verify_citation` (the re-verification exact-equality check) — found by tracing every caller of quote-matching logic before writing anything, specifically to avoid fixing the symptom in one place while leaving the same byte-exact assumption live in the other three (which would have re-broken citation building for a segment the verifier had just accepted). New `app.citations.model.find_verbatim_quote(quote, text) -> (start, end) | None` is the one shared primitive, used by all four: each run of whitespace in `quote` matches any run of whitespace in `text` (so a plain space, a newline, or multiple trailing spaces-then-newline from justified-text PDF extraction are all treated the same); every other character must still match exactly, in the same order. This is a narrowing of what counts as a false rejection, not a loosening of what counts as support — no paraphrase, no reordering, no synonym substitution, no fuzzy/similarity threshold is tolerated; a genuinely reworded quote (e.g. "heart rate" for "respiratory rate") still fails, covered by a dedicated test (`test_quote_does_not_tolerate_reworded_text`) precisely to guard against this fix ever drifting into an actual grounding weakening.
  - **Offsets now point at the real, possibly-longer span in the original text**, not `len(quote)` from wherever the first word matched — `build_citation`'s `quote_char_start`/`quote_char_end` bound the actual matched region (newlines and all) in the chunk's stored coordinates, so a UI highlighting `chunk_text[quote_char_start:quote_char_end]` shows the correct real span, and `verify_citation`'s re-check compares the excerpt and the stored `citation.quote` after the same whitespace normalization on both sides, rather than requiring byte equality between a (possibly wrapped) raw excerpt and a (always unwrapped) stored quote. `Citation.quote` itself still stores exactly what was passed in (the model's own quote text, normal spacing) — not the raw, newline-containing excerpt — preserving the existing `cit.quote == quote` contract `tests/test_build_citation.py` already relied on.
  - **Verified two ways.** (1) Targeted unit tests reproducing the exact failure shape (a chunk with a mid-sentence line-wrap newline, some with extra trailing whitespace before it, matching real PDF-extraction behavior) added at all three test layers (`tests/test_grounding_verifier.py`, `tests/test_citation_model.py`, `tests/test_build_citation.py`) — plus one deliberately-still-failing case per layer confirming the fix doesn't tolerate an actually different word. (2) Rebuilt the `api`/`worker` images and re-ran the exact real query from DEVIATIONS #103 against the same real gateway and the same already-ingested document: `observed_outcome` changed from `escalated`/`grounding_failure` to **`well_supported`** — 7 claim segments released, 2 real `Citation` objects with real page numbers/section paths/char offsets/verbatim quotes resolving into the actual ingested WHO guideline text, correctly audited (`action=answer, outcome=well_supported`). This is the first real, end-to-end, released-and-cited answer this project has produced against a real model.
  - **An unrelated operational issue hit while re-verifying, not a code bug:** after recreating only `api`/`worker` (not `proxy`) to pick up the rebuilt image, every request through the proxy 502'd — nginx resolves an upstream hostname to a fixed IP once at worker-process start and does not re-resolve it by default, so `proxy` kept trying to reach the *previous* `api` container's now-dead IP. Fixed operationally (`docker compose restart proxy`), not by changing `deploy/nginx/nginx.conf` — a dynamic-resolution nginx config (`resolver` + variable-based `proxy_pass`) would avoid this but is more invasive than warranted for a `docker compose up -d <one-service>` workflow; noted here so a future session recognizes the symptom (502s immediately after recreating `api`/`worker` alone) rather than re-diagnosing it as a new bug.
- **Reversible?:** yes — `find_verbatim_quote` only widens what's accepted as a match along one narrow, well-tested axis (incidental whitespace-run differences); every existing test in all four touched files still passes unmodified, and the new tests specifically pin down that a real wording change is still rejected.

### 105. Every second+ turn in a conversation silently re-returned the first turn's answer — checkpointer `thread_id` was scoped to the wrong lifetime
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-15 / post-Phase-5 (operator browser-testing report)
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §10.1, ARCH §10.2, PRD-107
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** none — a real, severe functional bug, reported as "repeat queries with different cases do not update the initial text shown" while browser-testing the dockerized stack. Confirmed at the API layer first (bypassing the frontend entirely) before touching any code, specifically to rule out a rendering bug: two different questions submitted to the same `conversation_id` via direct `curl` returned byte-identical `segments` for the second question as the first.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **Root cause:** `app.api.routes.query.submit_query` and `app.agents.tasks.run_query` both passed `conversation_id` directly as the LangGraph checkpointer's `thread_id`. LangGraph's checkpointer persists the *entire* state dict per thread and merges each new `invoke()`'s input on top of whatever was last checkpointed for that thread — so a second `/query` call in the same conversation started from a state that already had `scope_label`, `final_answer`, `candidate_segments`, `candidate_citations`, `observed_outcome` etc. left over from the *first* turn. `app.agents.orchestrator.run` (visited twice per turn — once to classify, once to assemble) tells its two visits apart by `"scope_label" not in state`, documented in its own module docstring as relying on "what's already in state" — a check that is only correct if the state is genuinely empty at the start of a turn. With a conversation-scoped `thread_id` it was not: turn 2's very first orchestrator call already looked like turn 1's *second* visit, so `_classify_and_dispatch` never ran — no scope classification, no retrieval, no synthesis for the new question at all — and `_finalize` re-assembled turn 1's stale `candidate_segments` as if they answered it. `_route_from_orchestrator`'s `if "final_answer" in state: return "END"` then routed straight out, before the new `query` was ever looked at by anything.
  - **Fix: `thread_id` scoped per graph run, not per conversation.** New `app.agents.graph_runtime.new_turn_thread_id(conversation_id) -> f"{conversation_id}:{uuid.uuid4()}"`, used by both call sites (`submit_query`, `run_query`) instead of the bare `conversation_id`. Confirmed no interrupt/resume feature in this codebase needs a *stable* thread across multiple `invoke()` calls before making this change, not assumed: grepped for `interrupt`/`get_state`/`update_state` — none exist anywhere; the checkpointer's only real job here is mid-turn crash durability (`app.memory.checkpointer`, ARCH §4.3 "resume/inspect"), which is unaffected — each turn still gets its own consistent, inspectable stream of checkpoint rows, just no longer sharing them with any other turn's. `conversation_id` is kept as a readable prefix on the thread id (for grepping `memory.langgraph_checkpoint` rows by conversation) rather than dropped entirely.
  - **Not a redesign of conversation memory** — genuinely out of scope for this fix, flagged rather than silently expanded into: nothing in this codebase currently re-injects prior turns' Q&A into the synthesis prompt (`app/agents/prompts/guideline_synthesis.md` has no history placeholder) — "a follow-up question stays in context" (the frontend's own `QueryPage.tsx` docstring) is, as implemented, realized only through the `memory.conversation`/`message` DB rows already written by `prepare_query`/`assemble_and_persist_response`, not through LangGraph state continuity. Whether the graph itself *should* see prior turns for richer context is a real, separate design question this fix does not answer — it only makes the current (DB-row-based) design work correctly instead of being silently broken by a fresh graph run for every turn accidentally reusing stale computed values.
  - **Verified two ways.** (1) A new integration-level test (`tests/test_agent_graph.py::test_reusing_a_thread_id_across_turns_leaks_stale_state`) reproduces the bug directly at the graph level using a real `MemorySaver` checkpointer and the real `orchestrator` node — deliberately reusing one `thread_id` across two different queries and asserting the second incorrectly gets the first's `scope_label`/`final_answer` — this documents *why* the fix lives in the callers (the graph itself has no way to know a thread_id is being reused across logical turns) and would fail if anyone "fixed" this differently by changing the graph instead. A second new test (`tests/test_query_route.py::test_two_turns_in_one_conversation_get_distinct_thread_ids`) asserts the actual route layer now passes a fresh id each call. (2) Rebuilt `api`/`worker`, redeployed, and reproduced the *exact* original bug report live: two different real questions ("...immediate drying and stimulation..." then "...cord clamping timing in preterm babies...") posted to the same real `conversation_id` through the real proxy — before the fix, both returned the drying/stimulation answer; after, the second correctly returned real, distinct cord-clamping content, both `well_supported`, both correctly audited under the same `conversation_id`.
  - **A pre-existing test pinned the old, buggy behavior** and had to be corrected, not just left passing by accident: `tests/test_agents_tasks.py::test_run_query_invokes_graph_persists_and_returns_response_dict` asserted `thread_id == CONVERSATION_ID` exactly — updated to assert the new, correct contract (`thread_id != conversation_id`, prefixed by it).
- **Reversible?:** yes — a scoping change to an internal identifier never exposed in any API response or persisted row keyed by anything else; `conversation_id` itself, the actual cross-turn identity everything else (DB rows, the frontend, this response) keys off, is completely unchanged.

### 106. Real guideline PDF uploads silently 413'd through the proxy — nginx's default 1MB body-size limit
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-15 / post-Phase-5 (found while re-testing citation behavior against a richer corpus)
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-036, ARCH-038, PRD-106
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** none — a real, previously-unhit deployment gap. Every ingestion test earlier this session used the one ~390KB sample guideline PDF; ingesting the other two real, full-size sample PDFs (~15MB and ~4.9MB — realistic sizes for actual clinical guideline documents) through the real proxy failed with `413 Request Entity Too Large` before the request ever reached `api`.
- **Decision & rationale:** `deploy/nginx/nginx.conf` had no `client_max_body_size` directive, so nginx's own default (1MB) silently capped every upload — a real operator dropping in a normal-sized real guideline PDF (ARCH-038's own stated ingestion path) would hit this on effectively the first real document. Fixed with `client_max_body_size 100m;` at the `http` block level (covers both the `/api/` proxy location and everything else). No corresponding limit exists anywhere in the FastAPI/uvicorn stack itself (checked `app/api/routes/ingest.py`/`app/config.py` — neither has one), so this was the only place capping it.
  - **A live edit-to-running-container test surfaced a separate, real environment quirk, not a config bug**: editing the bind-mounted `nginx.conf` on the host and running `nginx -s reload` inside the already-running `proxy` container did **not** pick up the change — `docker compose exec proxy cat /etc/nginx/nginx.conf` kept showing the pre-edit content (confirmed via differing `md5sum`/mtime) even though `docker inspect`'s reported bind-mount source path was correct and the host file definitely had the edit. This points to a filesystem-sync boundary between this session's shell and the Docker daemon's own view of the host filesystem in this particular environment — a bind mount is not guaranteed live here the way it normally would be. **Fix: recreate the container** (`docker compose up -d --force-recreate proxy`) rather than rely on a live reload after a bind-mounted file edit — this forces Docker to freshly resolve the mount at container start, which did pick up the change. Noted here as a process point for this environment specifically: after editing any bind-mounted config (`nginx.conf`, `.env` is not bind-mounted so unaffected), recreate the consuming container, don't just signal/reload it in place.
- **Reversible?:** yes — one nginx directive, strictly additive (raises a cap, changes no other behavior).

### 107. A released answer could reference a citation with no `Citation` object behind it — over-citing segments weren't narrowed to what actually verified
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-15 / post-Phase-5 (operator browser-testing report: "citations are limited to three rather than showing all the guidelines cited")
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH-014, ARCH-015, PRD-011, PRD-016
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** the operator's report didn't specify exactly what "limited to three" meant, and the frontend (`CitationList.tsx`) has no slicing/cap at all — it renders every citation it's handed. Rather than guess, the actual code paths that decide which citations exist were traced end to end (`app.grounding.verifier._verify_claim_segment` → `citations_for_segments` → `app.agents.citation_verifier_agent.run`) before concluding anything, and a real, reproducible defect was found independent of the operator's exact live example (which involves a live model and could not be reproduced on demand — see below).
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **Root cause:** `_verify_claim_segment` marks a claim SUPPORTED/WEAK if **any** of its (possibly several) `citation_ids` resolves to a chunk that actually contains the quote — a deliberately lenient pass/fail rule, reasonable on its own since a model commonly over-cites (lists two or three plausibly-relevant sources per claim when only one genuinely contains the quoted text). But the segment's `citation_ids` list itself was passed through **unmodified** into the released answer — still naming every id the model originally listed, including ones that never verified. `citations_for_segments` (correctly) only builds a `Citation` object for ids that actually resolve and match, so an unverified id ended up in the answer's inline citation references with **no corresponding entry** in the `citations` array at all — a footnote reference in the answer text pointing at nothing, and a citations list that under-counts what the answer text appears to reference. Confirmed directly (not just theorized) with a standalone reproduction calling the real `citation_verifier_agent.run()`: a segment citing `["c1", "c2"]` where only `c1`'s chunk contained the quote was released with `citation_ids: ["c1", "c2"]` intact, while `candidate_citations` contained only `c1` — exactly the shape of bug the operator's report describes.
  - **Fix:** `SegmentVerdict` gained `verified_citation_ids: list[str] | None` — `_verify_claim_segment` now records the *actual* matching subset (not just whether one exists) on every SUPPORTED/WEAK verdict. `app.agents.citation_verifier_agent.run` uses it to narrow each kept claim segment's `citation_ids` to that verified subset before `citations_for_segments` ever runs — so a released segment can never name a citation_id that doesn't have a real `Citation` behind it, without changing which segments pass or fail (the lenient "any match" pass rule is untouched; only what a *passing* segment is allowed to claim citation support from is tightened).
  - **Not the same "3 citations" the operator saw, and said so rather than claiming it was**: this fix could not be reproduced against the operator's exact live scenario — retesting against a richer, freshly-ingested 3-document corpus (to more plausibly trigger the multi-citation-per-claim pattern this bug needs) instead consistently hit two other, separate, real, pre-existing conditions: (a) `conflicting_sources` escalations (a real, *correct* safety behavior — genuine guidelines disagreeing on a question — for several broad questions against this richer corpus, never reaching synthesis at all) and (b) the synthesis model occasionally producing not-quite-valid JSON when given a longer/more complex retrieved-source block (a distinct, pre-existing model-output-reliability limitation, unrelated to this fix, not addressed here — flagged, not silently expanded into). Neither blocks confidence in the fix itself, which is verified independent of live model behavior (below), but neither should be read as "the operator's specific report is now fully explained" — it may also have been one or both of these, and that's disclosed rather than assumed resolved.
  - **Verified via direct code-level reproduction and new tests, not a lucky live sample**: a standalone script calling the real `app.agents.citation_verifier_agent.run()` with the exact bug-triggering shape, before and after the fix, showing `citation_ids` change from `["c1", "c2"]` (dangling) to `["c1"]` (consistent with `candidate_citations`). New tests at every layer touched: `tests/test_grounding_verifier.py::test_over_cited_segment_verified_citation_ids_is_only_the_matching_subset` (the verdict-level primitive), `tests/test_citation_verifier_agent.py::test_over_cited_segment_drops_the_unverified_id` (the agent-level integration, asserting both the narrowed segment and the citations list). Every pre-existing test in both files passes unmodified — the fix changes what a released *over-cited* segment is allowed to claim, not any existing pass/fail decision.
  - **Also fixed a separate real deployment gap found while re-testing this** (nginx's 1MB default body-size limit blocking real-sized PDF uploads) — logged separately as DEVIATIONS.md #106, not folded in here since it's an unrelated proxy-config issue, not a citation-logic one.
- **Reversible?:** yes — narrows what a passing segment is allowed to claim, changes no pass/fail decision anywhere; every existing test in both touched files/agents passes unmodified.

### 108. Citation labels shown to a reader aren't sequential (c1, c2, c7) — real, correct data, fixed as a display-only renumbering
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-15 / post-Phase-5 (operator report, live example: "What does the guideline recommend for a newborn admitted with low birth weight, apnoea and fever?" — citations rendered as c1, c2, c7)
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-011, PRD-107, ARCH-014
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** whether this is a backend data bug or a frontend display issue — traced before assuming either. `app.grounding.verifier._label_maps` assigns `c{i+1}` by the chunk's **retrieval-rank position** in that turn's `retrieval_snapshot` (`c1` = top-ranked retrieved chunk, ..., up to `c{TOP_K}`), matching the same positional labels `app.agents.guideline_synthesis_agent._build_sources` puts in the model's own prompt. A model citing only its 1st, 2nd, and 7th-ranked retrieved chunks produces exactly `c1`, `c2`, `c7`, correctly — DEVIATIONS #107 (same day) already confirmed no citation_id is dangling/fabricated. This is real, accurate data, not corruption — but a reader has no way to know "c3–c6 were retrieved but simply not cited" versus "something is missing," and it reads as broken.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **Fixed as a presentation-layer renumbering, not a backend response-shape change.** New `frontend/src/citations.ts::renumberForDisplay(segments, citations)` — a pure function remapping citation labels to sequential order of first appearance in the released segments (`c1, c2, c7` → `c1, c2, c3`), consistently across both the inline `[cN]` footnote references and the citations list, called once in `AnswerView.tsx` immediately before rendering. The backend response, the persisted `memory.message.citations` row, and anything else consuming the raw API are **untouched** — retrieval-rank labels stay the ground truth for tracing an answer back to retrieval logs/debugging, which sequential display numbers would destroy if pushed into the API contract itself. This keeps the fix minimal-blast-radius: zero backend/grounding/audit code touched, a pure UI-only transform.
  - **No frontend test runner exists in this repo** (`package.json` has no `test` script; no `vitest`/`jest` dependency; no `*.test.ts` files anywhere — an established, previously-disclosed gap, not something to silently backfill by introducing a whole test framework for one utility). Verified instead: (1) a standalone Node script running the exact same logic byte-for-byte, confirming `[c1, c2, c1, c7]` across four segments renumbers to `[c1, c2, c1, c3]` with the citations list remapped and reordered to match; (2) `npm run build` (`tsc -b` — full type-check) and `npm run lint --max-warnings 0`, both clean.
  - **Not addressed here, flagged separately to the operator**: a second, distinct report in the same message — that the answer for this specific, three-finding scenario reads as generically listing per-topic guideline content rather than an integrated, scenario-specific assessment. Investigated (not dismissed): retrieval genuinely only surfaced separately-topical passages (low-birth-weight/"small and sick newborn" care under one citation, apnoea/CPAP under another) with no single source passage addressing all three named findings together, and the synthesis agent is deliberately barred (ARCH §10.2, `guideline_synthesis.md` rule 1 "report, do not direct") from synthesizing those into one combined, patient-specific management narrative — doing so would cross into SCOPE-2.3 (autonomous next-step recommendation from patient-shaped input), a hard boundary (CDS-FUTURE.md). Whether/how to address this — e.g. a purely-organizational prompt change that groups reported content by named finding without ever synthesizing a combined judgment — was put to the operator directly rather than decided unilaterally, since any change to `guideline_synthesis.md` sits adjacent to a protected boundary.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a new, isolated frontend module and a two-line call-site change in `AnswerView.tsx`; no backend, schema, or persisted-data change of any kind.

### 109. Synthesis prompt taught to preserve a source's own combined-findings classification — operator-approved, verified against a real guideline's own intersection content
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-15 / post-Phase-5 (operator follow-up to DEVIATIONS #108's open question: "What about looking at the intersection of topics (e.g. low birth weight + apnoea + fever)?"; explicit go-ahead given for a `guideline_synthesis.md` change)
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §10.2, PRD-107, SCOPE-1 (CDS-FUTURE.md boundary)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** whether "the intersection of topics" was asking the AI to *synthesize* a combined assessment across separately-retrieved passages (which would be SCOPE-2.3 — explicitly out of scope, not attempted) or asking whether the *guidelines themselves* already document the combination, in which case reporting that faithfully is ordinary SCOPE-1 work. Investigated before writing any prompt text, not assumed: searched the ingested corpus directly for chunks mentioning both `apnoea` and `fever`, and found a real one — the Kenya "Comprehensive Newborn Care Protocols" has an "Assessing for possible Bacterial Infection" classification listing `Apnoea` as one of several signs that, together, map to "Severe neonatal sepsis" with a specific management plan, and a temperature range (covering fever) in the next, less-severe tier. Confirmed retrieval already finds this exact chunk (present in the fused dense+sparse candidate pool, `bb87ad6b-2782-482b-bc1d-bb9e35982ca2`) and that it had already been cited at least once in an earlier real answer (the "Oxygen if SpO2 below 90%" line the operator's very first pasted example included is verbatim from this chunk) — so the content existed and was reachable; the synthesis agent just wasn't reporting its classification structure coherently, extracting isolated fragments instead.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **New rule 7 in `app/agents/prompts/guideline_synthesis.md`**: when a **single** source passage groups several findings under one named classification/severity category with its own management, keep that grouping visible in the answer (a `framing` segment naming the category, followed by the `claim` segments for its findings/management) instead of reporting an isolated line with the category name dropped. Two guardrails kept the change from drifting toward SCOPE-2.3, both stated explicitly in the rule: (1) never combine findings/categories/management drawn from **different** source passages into a classification that doesn't appear as such in any single source — this stays "report one passage's own structure," never "synthesize a new connection across sources"; (2) never state or imply that the scenario in QUESTION *meets*, *satisfies*, or *falls under* the classification — the model may note that a named finding from QUESTION appears in the passage's own listed criteria (still a description of the source text), but deciding whether a specific case meets guideline criteria is explicitly named as the excluded clinical-judgment step this system does not take.
  - **First version of the rule caused a real, measurable regression, caught by live testing, not assumed away.** Drafted first as "report that classification as **one coherent claim**" — which pushed the model toward a single oversized segment with one very long verbatim quote (rule 4's per-claim quote requirement scaled with an entire multi-tier classification block instead of one line). Live-tested against the real gateway: the model's JSON output started getting cut off mid-string (`"Unterminated string"` parse failures) on 2 of 3 identical live attempts through the actual `/query` endpoint. Diagnosed before revising, not guessed at: reproduced the exact rendered prompt directly against the gateway (bypassing the agent's retry/parsing layer) and inspected the raw response's `finish_reason`/`usage` — `finish_reason: "stop"` (not `"length"`) with `completion_tokens: 1079` against a `num_predict` default of 4096 confirms this specific successful run was nowhere near any real token budget, and the identical direct call was fully reproducible (same prompt → same complete output, every time) — so the occasional truncation seen through the real `/query` endpoint is not this prompt wording deterministically overflowing a budget; it correlates with call-to-call variation in what retrieval/reranking hands the model (a pre-existing characteristic — the same class of malformed-JSON failure was already observed against the richer 3-document corpus in DEVIATIONS #103/#107, before any prompt change existed). Revised the rule anyway, since even without being the deterministic cause, encouraging one oversized quote was a real, avoidable risk factor: rewritten to require the classification's *connection* stay visible (framing segment naming the category + separate claim segments) while explicitly keeping each claim's quote "short, precise... not the whole block," matching the quote-length behavior that was already working reliably before this change.
  - **Verified live against the real gateway and the real ingested guideline, not just re-read for plausibility**: the operator's exact question, resubmitted through the real `/query` endpoint after the revision, produced (on the attempt that completed normally) a `well_supported` answer with the target chunk (`c7`) cited and claims explicitly connecting apnoea *and* fever to the guideline's own "severe neonatal sepsis" classification and management — properly hedged as reported content ("Per Comprehensive Newborn Care Protocols, if a newborn has apnoea (listed as a sign of severe neonatal sepsis), the recommended management includes...") — never asserting the described newborn meets that classification. This is the intended, verified improvement.
  - **Disclosed, not hidden: reliability is not 100% for this specific query, and that predates this change.** Across repeated live attempts (both before and after this prompt revision), the same query intermittently escalates with a `grounding_failure` from malformed/truncated model JSON — confirmed via direct reproduction to be a real, pre-existing gateway/retrieval-variability characteristic, not a new defect this change introduced, and not something fixable from this codebase without visibility into the operator's own gateway internals (its generation/response behavior under whatever conditions produce a shorter vs. longer output for slightly different retrieved-source content). When it happens, the system's existing safety behavior is unaffected and correct: a malformed synthesis output is never shown as a partial or garbled answer — it escalates for clinician review every time, exactly as designed.
  - **No test suite exercises `.md` prompt template content directly** (confirmed: no test file references `guideline_synthesis.md` or `_load_template`), so this change carries no offline test coverage of its own — `make lint`/`make test`/`make typecheck` are unaffected by a markdown-only change and were re-confirmed clean regardless, as a matter of course, not because they'd catch a wording regression.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a single new rule in one prompt template; no schema, route, or grounding-gate logic touched. The classification-preservation behavior is opt-in in the sense that it only changes output when a source passage actually contains this kind of grouped-findings structure; ordinary single-fact claims are unaffected.

### 110. The real root cause of the intermittent `grounding_failure`/"model output is not valid JSON" escalations: `parse_segments` never stripped a markdown code fence
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-15 / post-Phase-5 (operator report: the exact query from DEVIATIONS #109 returning `Synthesis output could not be parsed into valid answer segments: model output is not valid JSON: Unterminated string starting at: line 1 column 7058`)
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §8.2, PRD-107
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** DEVIATIONS #103/#107/#109 had each independently observed this same class of failure and, each time, disclosed it as a "pre-existing, intermittent, likely gateway/retrieval-variability characteristic" without being able to pin down a concrete, fixable cause — a real diagnostic gap, not resolved by re-asserting it again. This time, rather than reproduce-and-disclose once more, the actual raw model output was captured and inspected directly.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **Root cause, found by looking at the actual bytes, not inferred:** called the real gateway directly (bypassing the agent's retry loop) with the operator's exact query's real rendered prompt, captured the raw `content` string to a file, and read it. It started with `` ```json\n[...` `` and ended with `` ...]\n``` `` — the model (an Ollama-served local model, per DEVIATIONS #103's earlier finding) wraps its structured output in a markdown code fence, a well-known, common serving-side habit for locally-hosted models regardless of prompt instructions. `app.grounding.segments.parse_segments` called `json.loads(text)` on the **raw** string with **no fence-stripping of any kind** — `json.loads('` + "```json..." + `')` fails immediately with `Expecting value: line 1 column 1 (char 0)`. Confirmed deterministic and 100% reproducible: 6 identical direct calls (temperature=0, same backend `ollama-primary` every time) all produced the identical fenced content and identical parse failure. The *different*-looking errors seen in earlier sessions (`"Unterminated string starting at ... column 7058"`, `"Expecting property name enclosed in double quotes"`) are the same underlying fenced-output habit interacting with `json.loads`'s error reporting differently depending on exactly where in the (also-fenced) text the parser gives up, and/or a fenced response that also happened to be truncated for unrelated reasons — not evidence of a separate, deeper truncation bug as previously hypothesized. `app.agents.guideline_synthesis_agent`'s own single retry (`"STRICT: reply with ONLY the JSON list."` appended on a parse failure) does not reliably suppress this habit either — at temperature 0 the retry can reproduce the identical fenced output and fail identically, exhausting `_MAX_ATTEMPTS=2` and escalating.
  - **Fixed at the parsing layer, not the prompt** — this is a serving-side output-formatting habit, not a content-correctness problem an "Output format" prompt rule is a reliable way to suppress (already tried once, indirectly, via the existing "STRICT" retry suffix, and it doesn't reliably work). New `app.grounding.segments._unwrap_code_fence`, applied to real string model output before `json.loads`: a regex (`` ```(?:json)?\s*(.*?)\s*``` ``, `DOTALL`) extracts the fenced content if a complete fence (open **and** close) is present; text with no fence, or a fence that never closes (a genuinely truncated response), passes through unchanged and still correctly fails to parse — a real truncation is not something this fix papers over, it only stops a *complete*, valid, merely-fenced response from being wrongly treated as malformed. Precedent for this style of lenient extraction already existed in this codebase (`app.agents.citation_verifier_agent._gateway_entailment_fn`'s `re.search(r"\{.*\}", ...)` for the NLI-check response) — applied here for the first time to the segment-parsing path, which had none.
  - **Verified four ways, not just re-read for plausibility.** (1) The exact real fenced content captured live was fed through `parse_segments` before and after the fix — before: `SegmentParseError`; after: 8 well-formed segments, structurally valid, explicitly connecting both apnoea and fever to the Kenya guideline's "severe neonatal sepsis"/"neonatal sepsis" classification (DEVIATIONS #109's intended content, silently discarded by the parse failure every time it had actually been produced). (2) New unit tests (`tests/test_grounding_segments.py`) for a `` ```json `` fence, a bare `` ``` `` fence with no language tag, and a fence with surrounding whitespace — all existing tests in the file still pass unmodified. (3) `make lint`/`make test` (496 passed, up from 493)/`make typecheck` (same pre-existing 10-finding baseline) all clean. (4) Rebuilt `api`/`worker`, redeployed, and ran the operator's exact query live **4 times in a row**: `well_supported`, 11 segments, 3 real citations, every single time — zero parse failures, where before this fix roughly 2 of every 3 live attempts on this query failed this way.
  - **Not fully resolved, disclosed rather than overclaimed:** the four successful live runs above did not all cite the same chunks — retrieval/reranking still has real call-to-call variance for this specific multi-finding query (already disclosed in DEVIATIONS #109), so whether the "severe neonatal sepsis" classification chunk specifically gets retrieved and cited still varies run to run. That is a separate, pre-existing retrieval-ranking-consistency question, not this bug — this fix's scope is "a well-formed, complete model response is never wrongly discarded because it happened to be fenced," which is now true regardless of which chunks retrieval hands the model.
- **Reversible?:** yes — one new, narrowly-scoped regex-based unwrap applied only to real string model output, falling through unchanged for anything that isn't a complete, closed code fence; no change to what counts as valid/invalid JSON once unwrapped, no change to any grounding/citation/scope logic.

### 111. A second, different `grounding_failure` shape on the same query: the model ignoring the JSON schema entirely, writing prose with inline `[c1]` markers — fixed with a stronger retry, a few-shot example, and one more attempt
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-15 / post-Phase-5 (operator report, same query as DEVIATIONS #110, a different raw failure this time — flattened prose ending in inline `[c1]`/`[c2]`/`[c3]` markers, not a code-fenced JSON list)
- **Requirement ID(s):** ARCH §8.2, PRD-107, SCOPE-1
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** whether this was the *same* bug as #110 resurfacing (meaning that fix was incomplete) or a genuinely different failure mode. Checked before assuming either: the reported raw text has no code fence at all and isn't JSON in any form — plain narrative sentences each ending in a bracketed citation number, no `"type"`/`"text"`/`"quote"` keys anywhere. `_unwrap_code_fence` (#110) correctly does nothing here (there's no fence to unwrap) and correctly still fails to parse — #110's fix was not wrong, this is a distinct failure mode: the model ignoring the required JSON schema altogether, not wrapping valid JSON in markdown.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **Confirmed the existing single retry was not sufficient**, before making a fix: `guideline_synthesis_agent.run()`'s escalation message reflects `last_error` from the *last* attempt, and `_MAX_ATTEMPTS` was 2 — so this prose output was what the model produced even after the existing `"STRICT: reply with ONLY the JSON list."` retry suffix. The instruction wasn't being ignored by omission (attempt 1 only); it was ignored twice.
  - **Ruled out a fallback parser for this prose+bracket shape as unsafe, not just inconvenient.** Building a fallback to convert `"...sentence.[c1]"` into a claim segment was considered and rejected: this format carries no verbatim quote at all, only a paraphrase and a bracket. Accepting it would mean either (a) treating the paraphrase itself as the "quote" — which `find_verbatim_quote` would then correctly reject as not-actually-verbatim (no improvement), or (b) having the *system* invent/select a supporting excerpt from the cited chunk on the model's behalf — which would mean the grounding gate is no longer verifying a quote the model committed to, undermining exactly the property ARCH §8.3's citation-verifier exists to guarantee. The right fix is getting the model to reliably produce a real quote in the first place, not working around the absence of one.
  - **Three changes, layered, since no single one was assumed sufficient on its own:**
    1. **`_MAX_ATTEMPTS` raised 2 → 3** (`app/agents/guideline_synthesis_agent.py`) — one more chance, at the cost of extra latency only on the already-failing path.
    2. **The retry suffix rewritten to name the failure mode directly**, not just restate the instruction more tersely: explicitly says "Do not write prose. Do not use inline citation markers like [c1] in running text. Do not use markdown or a code fence," plus a literal inline JSON example — since a model that already ignored a positive-only instruction ("reply with ONLY the JSON list") twice needs the specific wrong behavior named, not a rephrasing of the right one.
    3. **A concrete few-shot example added to `guideline_synthesis.md`'s "Output format" section** — a complete, realistic 2-segment JSON example with an explicit "note what this example does NOT do" callout (no prose, no inline brackets, nothing outside the array). Few-shot examples are a well-established way to improve structured-output compliance in smaller/local models; this codebase had never had one for this prompt. Checked whether the gateway itself offers a native JSON-mode/schema-constrained decoding parameter first (would have been the more robust fix, constraining generation instead of hoping the model complies) — re-inspected `ChatCompletionRequest`'s full schema (DEVIATIONS #103's original source): `model, messages, temperature, top_p, top_k, seed, num_predict, repeat_penalty, repeat_last_n, num_ctx, stream, metadata` — no `format`/`response_format`/`json_schema` field exposed by this gateway's own API surface, so a prompt/parsing-level fix is what's actually available here, not a request-parameter one.
  - **The few-shot example immediately caused a real, observed side effect, caught by live testing, not assumed away**: the model started writing "Guideline X recommends…" *literally* in every claim — copying the example's own placeholder title verbatim instead of substituting the real retrieved document's title (rule 1's own pre-existing illustrative phrases, "Guideline X recommends…"/"Per [source]…", turn out to have had the same latent literal-copying risk all along — just less consistently triggered before a concrete JSON example reinforced one exact phrasing so strongly). Fixed by rewording rule 1 to explicitly require the **actual document title shown in SOURCES** and explicitly forbid reusing "Guideline X"/"[source]" literally, and rewriting the few-shot example to use an obviously-fictitious title ("Example Fever Management Guideline") with an explicit callout that it must never be reused — verified live afterward that the model correctly substitutes the real title ("Comprehensive Newborn Care Protocols recommends…").
  - **Verified live, end to end, after both rounds of fixes**: the operator's exact query, run 5 times through the real endpoint after the retry/example fix — 5/5 `well_supported`, identical structure, the combined "severe neonatal sepsis" classification (`c7`, DEVIATIONS #109) cited in every run this time (not just some, as in #110's verification); then, after the document-title fix, run 3 more times — 3/3 `well_supported`, correctly naming "Comprehensive Newborn Care Protocols" throughout, still correctly connecting apnoea and fever to the classification.
  - **Offline test coverage added**, matching this codebase's discipline of testing the actual failure shape, not just a generic one: `tests/test_guideline_synthesis_agent.py` gained `test_prose_with_inline_citation_markers_is_rejected_not_parsed` (the exact reported shape, confirmed to still correctly escalate rather than be accepted) and `test_third_attempt_succeeding_recovers` (the new third attempt actually gets exercised); `test_malformed_output_retries_then_escalates` updated for the new attempt count (`calls["n"] == 3`, was `2`) — the only test that pinned the old value.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a retry-count constant, a retry-suffix string, and prompt wording; no schema, grounding-gate, or citation logic touched. `_MAX_ATTEMPTS=3` costs additional gateway calls only on inputs that would otherwise have escalated anyway.

### 112. The 11-domain rubric was an invented placeholder, never sourced from a real rubric document — replaced with the operator's authoritative rubric text
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-15 / post-Phase-5 (operator supplied the complete, authoritative 11-domain rubric text verbatim, with full 5-point Likert anchor descriptions for every domain, and the clear implication it should replace what was implemented)
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-040, PRD-041, ARCH §14.1
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** the operator's rubric text numbers its domains I through X then XII — skipping XI, 11 domains total, not 12 — and gives no indication of which domains (if any) should be treated as a required minimum for a rating to count; the earlier placeholder set had picked 4 "required" domains (`accuracy`, `safety`, `contextual_appropriateness`, `clarity`) with no operator input either. Two judgment calls were needed to implement this as working code, both logged rather than made silently:
  1. **Ordinal sequencing.** Assigned sequential ordinals 1-11 in the order the operator's text presents the domains (I→1 ... X→10, XII→11), rather than literally preserving "12" as a gap in the stored `ordinal` column — nothing in the source text suggested the numbering gap was meaningful (no domain "XI" is referenced or implied anywhere in the supplied text), and a literal gap would only complicate `RUBRIC_DOMAINS`' own internal consistency assertions for no benefit.
  2. **Which 4 domains are `required=True`.** The operator's text does not mark any domain as required. Chose to keep the same *count* (4, matching the prior placeholder set and `RatingRoundRequest`'s existing `min_length=len(RUBRIC_DOMAIN_CODES)` behavior, which requires all 11 regardless) and the same *relative role* as the four the placeholder set had chosen: `medical_consensus_alignment` (~ old `accuracy` — core factual/guideline-alignment check), `extent_of_harm` (~ old `safety`), `clear_communication` (~ old `clarity`), `local_context_understanding` (~ old `contextual_appropriateness`). This is a genuine guess at operator intent, not a derived fact — flagged explicitly so it can be corrected if the operator has an actual required-domain policy in mind. (Note: today, `required` is reference metadata only — `RatingRoundRequest.scores` already requires exactly one score per domain for *all 11*, so this flag has no live behavioral effect yet; it exists for the future "brief"/partial-rubric workflow ARCH §14.1 anticipates.)
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **Full replacement, not a merge or an additive set.** The old 11 domains (`accuracy`, `groundedness`, `completeness`, `safety`, `scope_adherence`, `contextual_appropriateness`, `clarity`, `relevance`, `uncertainty_handling`, `missing_info_handling`, `bias_equity`) were never sourced from a real, citable rubric — they were this codebase's own invention during earlier phase work, filling a requirement (PRD-040: "an 11-domain rubric") that named a domain *count* and *scale* but not actual domain content. The operator's supplied text is the first real, authoritative source for what the 11 domains actually are, so it replaces the placeholder outright rather than living alongside it.
  - **`app/rubric/domains.py` rewritten**: all 11 `RubricDomain` entries replaced — new `code` (snake_case, chosen by this codebase, e.g. `medical_consensus_alignment`, `question_comprehension`, `knowledge_recall`, `logical_reasoning`, `irrelevant_content`, `information_omission`, `extent_of_harm`, `likelihood_of_harm`, `clear_communication`, `local_context_understanding`, `demographic_bias`), `name`, `definition`, and all 5 `anchor` strings per domain transcribed verbatim from the operator's text (no paraphrasing). Domains VII (`extent_of_harm`) and VIII (`likelihood_of_harm`) keep the operator's own anchor-label direction (1 = Severe/Very High = worst ... 5 = None/Very Low = best) — noted explicitly in the module docstring since it reads as reversed relative to the other 9 domains' "1 = worst, 5 = best" phrasing at a glance, even though the underlying 1=worst/5=best score convention is unchanged.
  - **Fixed a pre-existing, unrelated drift risk found while doing this**: `app/schemas/rubric.py` had its own, independently hardcoded `RUBRIC_DOMAIN_CODES` tuple — a duplicate of `app/rubric/domains.py`'s, previously kept in sync only by hand (a latent bug: nothing enforced they matched, and `tests/test_rubric.py::test_domain_codes_match_between_schema_and_reference` only caught it after the fact, not at import time). Changed `app/schemas/rubric.py` to `from app.rubric.domains import RUBRIC_DOMAIN_CODES` instead of redefining it — single source of truth going forward, not just for this change.
  - **Live DB reset, not migrated**: confirmed via direct query (`select count(*) from eval.rubric_rating`) that the live `eval.rubric_domain` table had zero dependent `rubric_rating`/`rating_round`/IRR/`result_archive` rows referencing the old codes before touching it — a clean reset, not a data-loss risk. `scripts/seed_db.py::_seed_rubric_domains` upserts by `code`, so re-running it against entirely new code strings only *adds* the new 11 rows without removing the old 11 (an upsert can't delete what it doesn't match) — confirmed this gap live (22 rows present after reseeding) before asking the operator, who explicitly approved deleting the 11 old orphaned rows (a raw `DELETE` on a live table, which this environment's own tooling flags for explicit confirmation before a bulk-delete regardless of author intent); confirmed 11 rows remain afterward, matching the new domain set exactly.
  - **Docs updated in the same change**: `ARCHITECTURE.md` §14.1's domain table and surrounding prose fully rewritten to the new domains; two other places in `ARCHITECTURE.md` (§17.5 threat table, §21 open-risks list) that named the old `scope_adherence` domain specifically were reworded — the new operator-supplied rubric has **no domain dedicated to reported-content/non-directiveness scope-adherence specifically**, so those two references now correctly describe that control as deterministic (prompt + wording filter), not rubric-scored, rather than pointing at a domain that no longer exists. `ARCHITECTURE-ESSENTIALS.md`'s domain-code summary line and `PRD.md`'s PRD-040 row updated to match.
- **Verified**: `make lint` (clean after `ruff format` on the rewritten file), `make test` (498 passed — up from 496; `tests/test_rubric.py`, `tests/test_rubric_tasks.py`, `tests/test_rubric_routes.py`, `tests/test_rubric_irr.py` updated for the new domain codes, no test logic changed beyond the literal code strings used as fixtures), `make typecheck` (same pre-existing, unrelated 10-finding baseline, none in files touched by this change). Live: rebuilt and force-recreated `api`/`worker`/`proxy` (the backend image bakes `app/` in rather than bind-mounting it, so a code-only change needs a rebuild, not just a container restart — same operational note as DEVIATIONS #102/#106), re-ran `scripts.seed_db`, and confirmed `GET /api/rubric/domains` returns exactly the 11 new domains with correct ordinals, definitions, and anchors, in order. Confirmed the route reads from `RUBRIC_DOMAINS` directly (`app/api/routes/rubric.py::list_domains`), not from the DB table, so the live orphaned-rows condition (before cleanup) had zero effect on served behavior — the DB table is reference/seed data, not the runtime source of truth for this endpoint.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a content replacement in one reference-data module plus a schema-file de-duplication; no migration, no schema-shape change (`RubricDomain`'s fields are unchanged), no grounding/citation/scope logic touched. The DB delete is the only non-trivially-reversible step, and it was explicitly operator-approved with zero dependent rows confirmed beforehand.

### 113. The rubric review queue had no writer — `eval.result` rows were never created outside the fixed test-set harness — closed with an auto-seed pipeline from de-identified records, plus a queue visibility/priority rule
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-15 / post-Phase-5
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-045, PRD-046, PRD-047, PRD-060, PRD-061, PRD-062, PRD-064, PRD-066, PRD-042; ARCH §14.2, §15 (step 2 record selection, step 7 diversity filter); carries forward DEVIATIONS #67 (steps 2/7 explicitly deferred — "need a populated, ingested corpus") and #79 (`eval.result` row creation out of scope for the review-queue router)
- **Ambiguous or undecided:** ARCH §14.2 documents only the `< IRR_MIN_RATERS` open-queue visibility cutoff, not any ordering or priority between a completely unrated result and one that already has 1-2 of the required 3 rating rounds. Resolved as: the queue only ever offers `open` results with 0-2 distinct rounds (a 3rd round is `can_archive`'s own trigger, so a `>=3`-round result should never reach this code path at all — `select_queue_items` defensively drops one anyway if it ever did); if **any** visible result has 1-2 rounds already ("in progress"), **only** those are offered, never a fresh 0-round one — finishing what's already partially rated takes priority over starting new work. Once no in-progress result remains, 0-round results become selectable again. Encoded as two functions in `app/rubric/workflow.py`: `list_queue_candidates` (the DB read — `open`, excluding results this rater already rated) and `select_queue_items` (the pure priority rule over that list, oldest-first within whichever bucket is offered — a deterministic tie-break, not a second priority tier).
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **The actual gap:** before this change, nothing in the codebase ever wrote an `eval.Result` row outside `app.eval.harness.run_harness` (the fixed synthetic test set only) — confirmed via `git grep 'Result(' app/` matching only the model definition. The rubric workflow's own `queue_state` transition (`app.rubric.workflow.submit_rating`, `not_queued -> open` on first rating) assumed a `Result` row already existed *before* any rating; nothing produced one. PRD-047 ("hard/adversarial cases included in the review queue") and PRD-066 ("auto Q/A used for... queue seeding when clinician volume is low") were therefore unimplementable — an empty queue was the only possible state via the code that existed.
  - **`app/eval/auto_seed.py::run_auto_seed_review_queue`** closes it: loads already-ingested, attested de-identified patient records (`app/eval/deidentified_source.py::load_deidentified_records`, `records.patient` where `data_class = 'deidentified'`, decrypted via `CryptoProvider`; never synthetic records — deliberately the same "real" de-identified source the operator's own dataset provides, not the Phase-1 fallback generator), reuses the existing 60/20/20 `expected_outcome`-stratified planner (`app.eval.question_gen.planner.allocate`, unchanged) and the existing scope-1-framed narrative generator (`app.eval.question_gen.generate.generate_question`, unchanged, including its no-fabrication validator), now for the first time actually enforcing the diversity filter ARCH §15.1 step 7 always specified but nothing implemented (`app/eval/question_gen/diversity.py::is_near_duplicate`, cosine similarity over the same `embed_texts` used for guideline-chunk embedding, deterministic plain code — no model call for the accept/reject decision itself, only for producing the embedding, consistent with CLAUDE.md §4's determinism rule), then runs each accepted question through the real query pipeline (`app.agents.graph_runtime.invoke_graph` — the exact same code path `POST /query` uses, not a separate/simplified one) and persists an `eval.Result` row with `queue_state="open"` **immediately**, not `"not_queued"`, so it is visible in the queue without any further step. Idempotent by construction: counts existing auto-generated `Result` rows (via the new `EvalQuestion.provenance == 'auto_generated'` join, scoped to rows that actually have a `Result` — the harness's own `generate_questions` task produces `eval_question` rows with no `Result` and is deliberately not counted) and only tops up to `QGEN_AUTO_SEED_COUNT`, so a container restart never duplicates the queue.
  - **Runs in the worker, triggered from API startup, not inline:** `app.main`'s `lifespan` hook enqueues `eval.auto_seed_review_queue_task` (Celery) on every startup rather than running it in-process, so N real LLM calls + N full graph invocations never block the API container from becoming healthy; the task itself fails soft (placeholder `MODEL_ID`, or zero de-identified records ingested yet, both log a warning and return rather than raising into Celery's retry loop) and is gated by `QGEN_AUTO_SEED_ENABLED` (default `true`).
  - **`records.patient` read scope:** `load_deidentified_records` opens its session with no `patient_scope` GUC set, deliberately — the RLS policies on `records.*` (ARCH-034) allow all rows when that GUC is unset, and this is a system/batch job that legitimately needs to read across many patients to pick generation source material, the same trust level as `scripts/seed_db.py`, not a clinician acting for one patient. Its output never reaches an HTTP response, never applies `record_field_policy` (that gate is specifically for a clinician-facing PHI read via `app.records.access.get_patient_fields`), and is sent only to the self-hosted `LLMGateway` as narrative-generation context — no new PHI egress path.
  - **`Result.answer_enc`'s AAD contract, formalized:** previously an inline byte-string literal duplicated (by convention, not by code) between the not-yet-existing writer and `app.api.routes.review_queue.get_queue_item`'s reader. Pulled into one function, `app.db.models.eval.result_answer_aad`, imported by both, so they cannot silently drift apart the way `app/schemas/rubric.py`'s duplicate `RUBRIC_DOMAIN_CODES` did (DEVIATIONS #112) — same class of bug, fixed before it could recur here.
  - **Queue route updated to the new priority rule:** `GET /review-queue` (`app/api/routes/review_queue.py::list_queue`) now calls `select_queue_items(list_queue_candidates(session, rater_id))` instead of a flat "all open, minus already-rated" read, and returns each candidate's `distinct_rater_count` in the response body (new field, additive — existing consumers reading `result_id`/`provenance`/`expected_outcome`/`observed_outcome` are unaffected). `app/api/routes/rubric.py`'s module docstring was extended, not duplicated, to point at `GET /review-queue` as the one real queue-listing endpoint (this module only ever had the per-result detail/rating endpoints).
  - **A real regression found and fixed while finishing this entry:** `tests/test_review_queue_routes.py`'s hand-rolled `_FakeSession.execute` (this file's existing pattern for testing a DB-touching route without a real database — it branches on `stmt.column_descriptions[0]["entity"]` to fake either a `RatingRound` or a `Result` select) had not been updated for `list_queue_candidates`'s new tuple-column query shape (`select(Result.id, Result.created_at, func.coalesce(...))`, a 3-column select whose first column's entity is still `Result`, so it fell into the existing whole-entity-`Result` branch and returned full `Result` objects where 3-tuples were expected) — `TypeError: cannot unpack non-iterable Result object` at `app/rubric/workflow.py:147`, the one test failure out of 517 found when this work was picked back up. Fixed by adding a `len(descriptions) > 1` branch to the fake that replicates `list_queue_candidates`'s actual filters in plain Python (open + not-already-rated-by-this-rater) and returns the same 3-tuple shape; not a production-code bug — `list_queue_candidates` runs correctly against a real SQLAlchemy `Session`/Postgres, only this file's fake needed to learn the new query shape, consistent with how it already special-cased the `RatingRound` select.
