  - **Reduced marker/line/font sizes** (e.g. chart 1/3 markersize 7-9→4-5, linewidth 1.5-2.5→1-1.8, tick/legend fontsize→7, title fontsize 12→9) since each panel's usable area shrank from the original standalone ~9.5in×5.5in to roughly 18cm×7cm (≈7in×2.75in) minus legend margin — the original sizes were tuned for the larger, single-chart canvas and would have been disproportionate/crowded at panel scale.
  - **Chart 2's legend shortened**: `"k={lbl} (dotted = RRF baseline at that k)"` (9 long entries) → `"k={lbl}"` with a short legend `title="dotted = RRF at that k"` instead — the reserved legend margin is now shared across all three panels' full figure width, not sized per-chart, so a verbose per-entry format that fit the old standalone 9.5in-wide chart 2 no longer reliably fits.
  - **Deleted the three old per-chart output files** (`chart1_k_vs_recall_by_alpha.png`, `chart2_alpha_vs_recall_by_k.png`, `chart3_alpha_vs_mrr.png`) from the repo and the live container's `reports/` directory; new single output is `retrieval_tuning_report.png`.
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, `tests/test_retrieval_tuning.py` (10 passed — no test touches `report.py`, confirmed by grep before this change; only `scripts/run_retrieval_weight_sweep.py` calls `generate_reports`, and its usage — iterate `paths`, print each — is unchanged by the one-vs-three-element list). Live — re-ran the real sweep inside the live `api` container against the same 67-question corpus, regenerated the combined PNG, copied it out and inspected it directly: three legible panels, correct A/B/C labels, legends fully inside the canvas (no clipping), exact target pixel dimensions confirmed via `PIL.Image.open(...).size` (4251×4960 @ 600dpi).
- **Reversible?:** yes — a rendering-layer refactor (how the existing computed data is drawn/saved); no change to `sweep.py`'s computation, no schema/API/production-path change. Reverting to three separate `Figure`s per chart is a `report.py`-only change.

### 130. Panel letters moved outside the plot area; "MRR" spelled out as "Mean Reciprocal Rank" on chart 3
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-17 / Phase 6, immediately following #129, same live stack
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-109/ARCH-040
- **What actually happened:** two follow-up requests on the combined report from #129: (1) move the A/B/C panel letters outside the plot area (they were inside each axes' top-left corner, with a backing patch to stay legible over data — #129), and (2) spell out "MRR" as "Mean Reciprocal Rank" on chart 3 (panel C)'s title and y-axis label.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - `_panel_label`: repositioned from inside the axes (`(0.015, 0.97)`, `va="top"`, with a `_SURFACE`-colored `bbox` backing patch) to outside it, above and slightly left of the top-left corner (`(-0.03, 1.16)`, `va="bottom"`). Outside the plot area, no data line can ever pass under the label, so the backing patch is no longer needed and was removed.
  - This needed more vertical room per panel than the #129 layout reserved (which only left space for the existing title above each axes, not a label above *that*): `generate_reports`'s `fig.subplots_adjust` changed `top` 0.965→0.93 (more margin above the topmost panel, A, which has no panel above it to borrow space from) and `hspace` 0.55→0.65 (more room between panels B and C's letters and the panel above them). Confirmed by re-rendering and visually inspecting: both the letter and the panel's own title fit fully inside the canvas on all three panels, no clipping at the figure's top edge.
  - `chart_alpha_vs_mrr`: `ax.set_ylabel(f"MRR@{MRR_K}")` → `f"Mean Reciprocal Rank@{MRR_K}"`; chart title likewise. Left `MRR_K`, the `mrr()` metric function, and other internal identifiers unchanged — the request was about the rendered chart text, not the code's own naming.
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, `tests/test_retrieval_tuning.py` (10 passed, unchanged — no test touches chart rendering or label text). Live — re-ran the real sweep inside the live `api` container, regenerated the combined PNG, copied it out and visually confirmed: all three panel letters sit clearly above and outside their respective plot areas with no overlap or clipping, and panel C reads "Mean Reciprocal Rank@24" in both its title and y-axis label.
- **Reversible?:** yes — a label-position change (with matching margin adjustments) and a display-string change; no schema, API, computation, or production-path change.

### 131. Biomedical embedding ablation (SapBERT / MedCPT vs. BM25) implemented and run for real — `PHASE2-EMBEDDING-ABLATION-PROPOSAL.md` approved and executed; one real bug found and fixed; real result recorded
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-17 / evidence-gathering for a possible future Phase 2 reopening (not itself a phase), approved same day
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-110, ARCH-041
- **What was implemented, matching the approved proposal:** `app/eval/model_ablation/` (`encoders.py` — SapBERT/MedCPT loading + `[CLS]`-pooling, with a `stub` mode for offline tests; `ablation.py` — brute-force in-memory cosine ranking over the full corpus + client-side RRF combine across three arms (`sapbert_bm25`, `medcpt_bm25`, `sapbert_medcpt_bm25`) plus a freshly-recomputed production-RRF reference; `report.py` — a 2-panel PNG, mirroring the Phase 6 report's palette/layout conventions), `scripts/run_model_ablation.py`, `tests/test_model_ablation.py` (9 tests, stub backend, no network). One additive `QdrantVectorStore` method, `scroll_all` (full corpus dump, payload-only, no vectors) — used only by this harness, not the `VectorStore` Protocol, same justification pattern as Phase 6's `single_vector_search`. New config, read only by this tooling, never by production: `MODEL_ABLATION_BACKEND`, `SAPBERT_MODEL_ID`/`_VERIFIED`, `MEDCPT_QUERY_MODEL_ID`/`MEDCPT_ARTICLE_MODEL_ID`/`_VERIFIED`.
- **Judgment calls made while implementing** (per proposal §10's commitment to log these the moment they're made):
  1. **RRF constant reused from `settings.rrf_k` (60)**, not a separately-invented value — matches `vectorstore.py`'s own documented assumption that 60 is "Qdrant's own internal constant" (DEVIATIONS #49). The client-side RRF here is not expected to reproduce Qdrant's fused scores number-for-number (there is no Qdrant collection to compare against for the two new channels), only to combine all three ablation arms consistently with each other and with the same constant production nominally uses.
  2. **`_bm25_rank`'s zero-score tail is `sorted()`, not left in whatever order Qdrant/`set` iteration happens to return.** A chunk with no lexical overlap with the query gets no score from the sparse query at all; those chunks must still get *some* deterministic rank for the RRF sum to be reproducible run-to-run.
  3. **The production RRF reference is recomputed fresh per question** (a real `store.hybrid_search` call), not copied from Phase 6's historical numbers as the original proposal (§5) suggested might be sufficient. Decided during implementation that reusing already-existing code for an always-current, always-consistent reference was simpler and more robust than embedding a specific historical number that could go stale — a refinement of the proposal, not a deviation from its intent (it explicitly called this "no new computation," which recomputing via an already-existing method still satisfies in spirit).
- **Real bug found only by real data, not the stub-backend tests:** `Encoder.encode`'s tokenizer call had no explicit `max_length`, relying on the tokenizer's own default — for at least one real guideline chunk (4481 tokens), that default did not truncate, and the forward pass crashed (`RuntimeError: size of tensor a (4481) must match size of tensor b (512)`) instead of silently truncating as intended. Fixed with an explicit `max_length=512` (the standard BERT-family position-embedding limit, matching the existing `reranker_max_length` convention elsewhere in this codebase) and batched encoding (`batch_size=16`, avoiding one single giant 314-text forward pass). The tiny fixture texts in `test_model_ablation.py` never exercised this path — same class of gap as several earlier "only real data found this" entries in this file (#122's gold-chunk gap, #122's chart y-range/legend bugs).
- **Model repo ids confirmed to exist; pooling method still not independently re-verified.** Both `SAPBERT_MODEL_ID` and the two `MEDCPT_*_MODEL_ID` placeholders resolved successfully against the real HuggingFace Hub (tokenizer + weights loaded without error) — the proposal's "best knowledge, unverified" ids turned out to be correct as repo ids. `[CLS]`-token pooling (this implementation's choice) was not independently re-checked against either model's current reference usage in this session; `*_VERIFIED` flags remain `false` until that specific check happens, per the proposal's own §6 standard (repo-id existence and "verified against the model's current card" are not the same claim).
- **Real result (informational, not a production change; 67 calibration questions, the same live 3-document guideline corpus, single run):** on MRR@24, **SapBERT+BM25 leads** (≈0.18) over the production RRF baseline (≈0.16, recomputed fresh this run — close to, not identical to, the ≈0.157 figure recorded in DEVIATIONS #123 from a different code path, as expected for two independently-computed RRF baselines); **MedCPT+BM25 underperforms it** (≈0.10); the 3-way **SapBERT+MedCPT+BM25 combination sits close to, slightly below, RRF** (≈0.14) — MedCPT's weaker showing pulls the 3-way blend down from where SapBERT alone lands. Recall@k: all three arms track closely with RRF through the swept range and converge to the same ≈0.68 ceiling by k≈50 (the corpus's own ceiling, per DEVIATIONS #123 — not a fusion-strategy effect). **Reading against proposal §8's bar**: SapBERT is the more promising of the two candidates on this single run, but N=67 over one small corpus, with no repeated splits or significance check, is not "robustly improves ... over the current RRF baseline" — this does **not** trigger a Phase-2-reopening proposal on its own. A natural next step, if this line of investigation continues, would be re-running with `[CLS]`-pooling independently verified and/or a mean-pooling alternative, and checking stability across a couple of random splits of the eval-question pool (the same robustness bar Phase 6's own §7 set) — not undertaken here, out of scope for this approved proposal.
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, `tests/test_model_ablation.py` (9 passed), full `make test` equivalent (547 passed, up from 538 — no existing test touched or broken), `mypy` at the same pre-existing baseline (only the already-accepted `pandas`/`seaborn` missing-stubs findings and the unrelated `app/logging.py` finding — no new findings). Live — real run against the live `dev` stack described above; one real PNG produced and visually reviewed (2 legible panels, panel letters outside the plot area matching the Phase 6 report's established convention, no data/legend collision).
- **Reversible?:** yes — everything here is new, additive code (`app/eval/model_ablation/`, one new `QdrantVectorStore` method, new config read only by this tooling, one new Makefile target, one new optional-dependency combination already covered by existing extras). Nothing in `app.retrieval.hybrid`/`vectorstore.hybrid_search` (the production path) was changed, no new Qdrant vectors were persisted, and no new `audit.audit_event` writes were added.

### 132. Panel B (MRR@24 by arm) changed from a plain bar chart to a dot plot with 95% bootstrap CI error bars (user request) — revealed the four arms' point estimates aren't actually distinguishable
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-17 / immediately following #131, same live stack
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-110/ARCH-041
- **What actually happened:** follow-up request to add 95% bootstrap confidence intervals to panel B and switch its mark from bars to a dot plot with error bars. This needed `run_ablation` to retain something it previously discarded: `mrr_rows` only ever stored each arm's *mean* MRR, not the per-question scores the mean was computed from — no CI is possible without them.
- **Decision & rationale:**
  - **Percentile bootstrap, not a normal-approximation interval.** `ablation._bootstrap_ci` resamples each arm's per-question MRR scores with replacement `_BOOTSTRAP_N=10,000` times, takes each resample's mean, and reports the [2.5, 97.5] percentiles of that distribution. Chosen over a normal/t-approximation because MRR scores are a bounded, discrete-ish, right-skewed quantity (mostly `0` or `1/rank` for small rank) — exactly the kind of distribution a normal approximation fits poorly, while the bootstrap makes no shape assumption.
  - **Fixed seed (`_BOOTSTRAP_SEED=1234`), not left to numpy's default entropy source.** Without it, regenerating the same report from the same underlying data would jitter the CI bounds slightly on every run — a report whose interval width changes for no reason other than re-running it would undermine trust in the number itself. Both the seed and the resample count are arbitrary-but-fixed judgment calls, not derived from anything.
  - **`AblationResult.mrr_rows` gained two fields** (`ci_low`, `ci_high`) rather than a parallel structure — kept the one-row-per-arm shape callers already expect, additive only.
  - **Chart mark**: `chart_mrr_by_arm` now draws `ax.errorbar` (the interval) plus `ax.scatter` (the point estimate) per arm, replacing `ax.bar` — same categorical color assignment and RRF-as-diamond/gray convention as the bar version, just a different mark for the same identity encoding. Title updated to name the interval (`"... (95% bootstrap CI)"`) so the chart is self-describing without needing the caption/DEVIATIONS entry to know what the whiskers are.
- **Real finding, only visible once uncertainty was shown (informational, not a production change):** all four arms' 95% CIs overlap substantially — SapBERT+BM25 ≈0.18 [≈0.13, ≈0.24], MedCPT+BM25 ≈0.10 [≈0.07, ≈0.14], SapBERT+MedCPT+BM25 ≈0.14 [≈0.10, ≈0.19], RRF production ≈0.16 [≈0.12, ≈0.22] (bounds read off the rendered chart, real run, same 67 questions and corpus as #131). No arm's interval excludes another's point estimate. This makes explicit, with an actual interval rather than just a caution in prose, what DEVIATIONS #131 already flagged qualitatively: SapBERT's higher point estimate on this run is not statistically distinguishable from the RRF baseline at this sample size — reinforcing, more rigorously than before, that this ablation does **not** meet proposal §8's "robustly improves" bar and does not trigger a Phase-2-reopening proposal.
- **Verified**: offline — `ruff check`/`ruff format --check` clean, `tests/test_model_ablation.py` (13 passed, up from 9 — 4 new: CI-bracketing check added to the existing aggregation test, plus determinism/degenerate/empty/realistic-n tests for `_bootstrap_ci` directly), full suite (551 passed, up from 547), `mypy` unchanged from #131's baseline. Live — re-ran the real sweep inside the live `api` container against the same 67-question corpus and regenerated the PNG; panel B visually reviewed (4 legible dot+whisker points, correct colors/markers, RRF still the gray diamond, no overlap with panel A, title names the interval).
- **Reversible?:** yes — two additive dataclass fields, one new pure function, and one chart function's mark; no schema, API, computation-of-existing-metrics, or production-path change.

### 133. Phase 5 (React frontend) reopened for a WCAG 2.2 AA contrast/accessibility pass — analysis only in this entry, no code changed yet
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-17 / Phase 5 reopened (originally closed, Checkpoint 5 approved 2026-09-14)
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-107 (query/citation UI), PRD-031/PRD-032/PRD-040 (rank mode + accept axis)
- **What happened:** operator explicitly asked to reopen Phase 5 and requested a contrast/accessibility assessment (WCAG 2.2 AA/AAA) of `frontend/src`, plus mobile Likert-rubric UX guidance referencing `frontend/mobile_mockup.html` (a design-intent fragment, not wired into the app — its `var(--surface-1)` etc. tokens are undefined anywhere in the repo).
- **Scope interpretation (judgment call):** treated "reopen Phase 5" as reopening the existing, already-approved React frontend for a contrast/accessibility remediation pass — not a rebuild. Delivered a code-grounded audit (real computed contrast ratios against colors actually in `frontend/src`, e.g. `#888`/`#999` text-on-white failures in `CitationList.tsx`, `EscalationQueue.tsx`, `ReviewQueue.tsx`, `AnswerView.tsx`; the native-radio-table anti-pattern in `RubricForm.tsx`/`AcceptAxisControls.tsx`; the total absence of any hover/focus-visible/active styling or CSS file in the frontend) plus concrete fixes, but did **not** apply any code changes or add new dependencies (Tailwind/Radix) in this pass — those were offered as recommendations pending operator confirmation, since introducing a CSS framework and a component-primitives library is a stack decision beyond a pure contrast-value fix and outside what "evaluate contrast and visibility" alone authorizes.
- **New checkpoint required**: Phase 5's status is now "reopened," not "complete," until the operator reviews the audit, decides which fixes (and whether Tailwind/Radix) to apply, the changes are implemented and tested, and a new Checkpoint 5 approval is given — per CLAUDE.md §2, never chaining into implementation/Phase 6 work off an old approval.
- **Verified**: N/A — no code changed in this entry (audit/analysis only).
- **Reversible?:** yes — this entry and the README status-row edit are documentation only.

### 134. Wired up Tailwind CSS v4 + Radix UI primitives; rebuilt the rank-mode rubric as a one-domain-per-screen wizard; fixed two real correctness bugs found only by driving the actual rendered wizard, not by inspection
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-17 / Phase 5 (reopened, DEVIATIONS #133), operator approved "go ahead" on the Tailwind/Radix proposal
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-031, PRD-032, PRD-040, ARCH §13.2
- **What was implemented:** `frontend/package.json` gained `tailwindcss` + `@tailwindcss/vite` (dev) and `@radix-ui/react-radio-group`, `@radix-ui/react-collapsible`, `@radix-ui/react-progress` (runtime, though the progress bar itself is a plain styled `div` — Radix's `Progress` wasn't actually needed once built, left installed rather than pulled back out for a one-line diff). `vite.config.ts` adds the `@tailwindcss/vite` plugin. `frontend/src/index.css` (new) declares the audited color tokens (DEVIATIONS #133 — `ink`/`ink-muted` replacing the old `#666`/`#888`/`#999`, `accent`/`accent-strong` teal, `danger` red reserved for `reject`/`out_of_scope`, the disclaimer banner's proven-AAA colors kept byte-for-byte) via Tailwind v4's `@theme`, plus a global `:focus-visible` rule. Every component that previously used inline `style={}` (`App.tsx`, `DisclaimerBanner.tsx`, `QueryPanel.tsx`, `AnswerView.tsx`, `AnswerSegments.tsx`, `CitationList.tsx`, `EscalationQueue.tsx`, `ReviewQueue.tsx`, `LoginPage.tsx`, `QueryPage.tsx`, `ReviewPage.tsx`, `EscalationsPage.tsx`) was converted to Tailwind utility classes against these tokens; a shared `components/ui/Button.tsx` centralizes hover/active/disabled/focus styling so it can't drift per call site again. `RubricForm.tsx` was rebuilt as a stepper (one domain per screen, collapsible definition + all 5 anchors, a collapsible "view answer and citations" panel holding `candidateAnswer`, a segmented 1–5 `RadioGroup` at 52px targets, a sticky bottom Back/Next bar with an "N/11 rated" count) ending in a dedicated, larger-format accept-axis step. `AcceptAxisControls.tsx` (shared with `EscalationsPage.tsx`'s standalone decision form) was rebuilt as large `RadioGroup` cards, `reject`/`out_of_scope` the only ones styled with `--color-danger`.
- **Two real bugs found and fixed by actually driving the rendered wizard** (a throwaway Puppeteer-over-real-Chrome harness against `vite dev`, seeded with the real 11 domains from `backend/app/rubric/domains.py`; not present in the final diff — this is process, not a shipped artifact):
  1. **Domain-rating `RadioGroup` silently stopped recording scores from the 2nd domain onward.** Passing `value={value?.toString()}` (`undefined` for an unrated domain) made Radix's `RadioGroup` fall back to *uncontrolled* mode the first time an unrated domain rendered; uncontrolled mode then *kept* the previously-checked item's value internally, so the next domain visually rendered with the *prior* domain's rating pre-"checked" (with no matching `scores` entry) — and clicking that already-"checked"-per-Radix item fired no `onValueChange`, since Radix saw no value change. A rater would appear to select a rating and nothing would happen, with no error. Fixed by always passing a defined string (`""` for unrated) — see the comment at `RubricForm.tsx`'s `DomainStep` `RadioGroup.Root`.
  2. **Clicking "Next →" on the 11th (last) domain silently auto-submitted the rating with the untouched default `full_accept` value, before the rater ever saw the accept-axis step.** The Next/Submit button occupies the same JSX tree position across the `onAcceptStep` ternary, so React patches the *same* underlying `<button>` DOM node's `type` attribute (`"button"` → `"submit"`) in place rather than remounting it. The browser resolves a click's native default action against the element's type *after* all JS handlers finish running, so the click that advanced `step` to the accept-axis screen — synchronously re-rendering that same node as `type="submit"` — had its own default action resolved against the *new* type, submitting the form immediately. Confirmed via a debug build: `onSubmit` fired with `step=11` (i.e. immediately after the transition) on every 11th-domain "Next" click, before any accept-axis interaction. This would have contaminated every multi-rater IRR submission with a spurious, never-chosen `full_accept` decision. Fixed by making the submit button `type="button"` with an explicit `formRef.current?.requestSubmit()` call, so it no longer depends on native submit-button click-dispatch timing.
  - A third suspected issue (accept-axis cards appearing to show a stale selected color in screenshots) was investigated with a computed-style dump and ruled out: it was `transition-colors`'s ~150ms CSS transition caught mid-animation by a zero-delay scripted screenshot, not a real bug — confirmed by re-sampling after a 300ms settle (exact expected token colors, one card selected at a time). Recorded here so a future session doesn't re-chase the same non-bug from a similarly fast script.
- **Verified**: offline — `npx tsc -b` clean, `npx eslint src --max-warnings 0` clean (0 errors/warnings), `npm run build` succeeds (73 modules, 13.80kB CSS / 219.75kB JS bundle). Live — real interaction testing against `vite dev` (real Chrome, CDP-driven, no mocks beyond stubbing the one `/rubric/domains` network call with the real seed data) covering: all 11 domains ratable and advancing correctly post-fix, definition/answer collapsibles opening, accept-axis card selection (all 4 options, including the danger-styled pair), partial_accept's edited-answer textarea, focus-visible keyboard navigation, and a correct final submit payload. Not run against the live backend/full docker stack (out of scope for a frontend-only accessibility pass; `getRubricDomains`/`submitRating` themselves are untouched pass-through calls in `api/client.ts`).
- **Known pre-existing, unrelated `npm audit` findings**: `esbuild`/`vite` (dev-server-only CORS advisory) and `react-router`/`react-router-dom` (open-redirect / SSR-hydration advisories) — both predate this change, both require a breaking major-version bump (`vite@8`, `react-router-dom@7`) to fix, and neither was introduced by the packages added here (`tailwindcss`, `@tailwindcss/vite`, `@radix-ui/*`, none of which `npm audit` flagged). Left as-is; flagging rather than silently upgrading unrelated deps beyond this task's scope.
- **Reversible?:** yes — additive dependencies and a rewrite scoped to `frontend/`; no backend, schema, or API contract change. `RatingSubmission`/`AcceptAxisValue` payload shapes are unchanged (verified via the real submit payload above), so `api/client.ts` and the backend routes it calls needed no changes.

### 135. Live-verified the #134 rewrite against the real `dev`-profile docker stack (real Postgres/Qdrant/gateway) — closes the "not run against the live backend" gap #134 left open; two real findings, one already-safe, one flagged not fixed
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-18 / Phase 5 (reopened), operator asked to "spin up the backend stack and test it live"
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-031, PRD-032, PRD-040, PRD-107
- **What was done:** `make up` (dev profile) against the existing, previously-migrated-and-seeded `pg-data`/`qdrant-data` volumes from an earlier session (11 rubric domains, seeded demo users, a populated `eval.result` review queue from a prior eval run already present — nothing new seeded); rebuilt only the `frontend` image (`docker compose build frontend`) to pick up the Tailwind/Radix changes and recreated that container. Confirmed `EMBEDDING_BACKEND=local` and `LLM_GATEWAY_URL=https://host.docker.internal:8443` point at the operator's real self-hosted gateway (already running, unrelated to this repo) — so this pass exercises real synthesis, not the offline stub. Drove the real app through the real `proxy` container (`https://localhost`, real TLS, real nginx) with the same real-Chrome/CDP approach as #134, this time against the actual running stack: dev-login as `reviewer1@example.dev`, a real `/query` submission, then a real `/review` queue item rated end-to-end through the rebuilt wizard and submitted for real (persisted a real `rating_round` + `accept_action` row).
- **Real findings:**
  1. **A real query returned `escalated`** (`"Synthesis output could not be parsed into valid answer segments: model output is not valid JSON: Unterminated string starting at: line 36 column 14 (char 1857)"`, `trigger_code: grounding_failure`) for a longer question. This is the grounding safety net working as designed — PRD-NFR-2/CLAUDE.md §3's "never a general-knowledge answer" held under a real, messy LLM failure mode (the real gateway's output was truncated mid-JSON), not a frontend bug; `AnswerView.tsx`'s escalation card rendered it correctly (danger-red border, trigger code, outcome, all legible). Root cause (likely a `max_tokens` ceiling on the real gateway call cutting off a longer synthesis) is a backend/gateway tuning question, out of scope for this frontend pass — not investigated further here, flagged for whoever next touches `LLMGateway`/synthesis prompt sizing.
  2. **A second, shorter real query returned `no_guideline`** rather than a citation-backed answer — also correct, conservative grounding behavior for a real corpus/gateway combination, not a bug.
  3. **`ReviewQueue.tsx`/`EscalationQueue.tsx`'s queue-list `<table>` is not mobile-responsive** — confirmed live at 390px width against a real, multi-row queue (12 real rows): the 5-column table (`Result` id / `Provenance` / `Expected outcome` / `Observed outcome` / action button) overflows the viewport and requires horizontal scroll; some columns render off-screen with no visual affordance that more content exists sideways. This is pre-existing table markup (unchanged in shape by #134's Tailwind conversion, which only swapped inline `style` for equivalent utility classes) — not a regression introduced this session, but a real gap in the same "mobile-first clinical tool" spirit as #133/#134's wizard work. **Fixed same-day, operator approved**: see #136.
- **Verified**: live, against the real stack described above — `alembic current` at head, dev-login working for `reviewer1@example.dev` (`clinician`+`reviewer` roles), `GET /rubric/domains` returning the real 11 seeded domains through the real proxy, a full rate-all-11-domains-then-accept-axis-then-submit pass against a real `eval.result` row succeeding with the correct payload and UI confirmation ("Rating submitted (accept axis: full_accept)."), and both query outcomes above rendering correctly. Stack left running (not torn down) at the operator's implicit continuation of this session; no repo files changed by this entry beyond this log (all test scripts lived under the session scratchpad, not the repo).
- **Reversible?:** yes — this entry is verification only; no application code changed. The stack can be stopped with `make down` at any time.

### 136. Fixed the #135 queue-list mobile overflow — `ReviewQueue.tsx`/`EscalationQueue.tsx` now render a stacked card list below `sm`, the existing table at `sm` and up
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-18 / Phase 5 (reopened), same session as #133–#135, operator approved fixing it now
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-042, PRD-047, PRD-107
- **What was implemented:** both components now render two markups gated by Tailwind's `sm:` breakpoint rather than one: a `sm:hidden` stacked-card list (id + provenance on one line, expected/observed — or trigger/opened for escalations — stacked below, a full-width action button in the thumb-reach zone, matching the wizard's card language from #134) and a `hidden sm:table` version of the original table, unchanged in shape. Both markups render off the same `items` array and the same `onSelect` callback — no duplicated data-fetching or state, just two presentations of it, same pattern as any other responsive-by-CSS component in this codebase (nothing is mounted/unmounted by JS on resize, so there's no state-loss risk crossing the breakpoint).
- **Verified**: offline — `npx tsc -b`, `npx eslint src --max-warnings 0`, `npm run build` all clean. Live — rebuilt the `frontend` image and recreated the container against the same running `dev`-profile stack from #135 (real, now-12-row-plus queue from repeated live testing this session); confirmed via `document.documentElement.scrollWidth > clientWidth` at 390px on both `/review` and `/escalations` (`false` on both — no horizontal overflow), confirmed the table is `display: table` (visible, not stuck hidden) at 1024px, and re-ran a full real rate-an-item-through-the-wizard-and-submit pass from the new card layout's "Rate this" button to confirm the card list wires up identically to the old table row (it does — same `onSelect(r.result_id)` handler, unchanged).
- **Reversible?:** yes — additive markup in two existing components, gated entirely by CSS breakpoint; no prop, state, or data-flow change.

### 137. Review-queue detail (`ReviewPage.tsx`): added a "Generated AI LLM response:" title and paragraph spacing between it and "Generated query:" (operator request)
- **Date / phase:** 2026-09-18 / Phase 5 (reopened), same session as #133–#136
- **Requirement ID(s):** PRD-107
- **What actually happened:** operator asked for visual separation between the "Generated query:" line and the answer content below it in the rank-mode result detail view, plus a title identifying that content as the model's output.
- **Decision & rationale:** `RankModeSection`'s detail block now uses `space-y-4` on its outer container (was: no gap management between the query line, the answer content, and `RubricForm` — they ran together with only whatever margin each child happened to bring on its own) and wraps the answer content (`AnswerSegments`, or the flat-text fallback + `CitationList`) in its own `<div>` headed by `<h5>Generated AI LLM response:</h5>`. Plain text label, not a rephrasing — "AI LLM response" is the operator's own wording, kept verbatim rather than editorialized (e.g. to "Answer" or "Synthesized response"), since it's their own review-tool vocabulary, not user-facing product copy elsewhere in the app.
- **Verified**: offline — `npx tsc -b`, `npx eslint src --max-warnings 0`, `npm run build` all clean. Live — rebuilt the `frontend` image, recreated the container against the same running `dev`-profile stack, opened a real review-queue item (real generated question + real LLM answer + real citation) at both 390px and 900px and visually confirmed: clear paragraph spacing between "Generated query:" and the new "Generated AI LLM response:" heading, spacing preserved down through the citation block and into the rubric wizard below.
- **Follow-up same session**: operator asked for the new heading to match "Generated query:"'s font size/face exactly. The initial `<h5 className="text-[13px] font-semibold">` was smaller and a different weight than the ambient paragraph text. Replaced with the same tag shape as "Generated query:" (a plain `<p><strong>…</strong></p>`, no size override), so both inherit the same ambient font. Verified live via `getComputedStyle` on both `<strong>` elements against the real running stack: identical `fontSize` (16px), `fontFamily`, and `fontWeight` (700).
- **Reversible?:** yes — presentation-only change to one existing component; no prop, type, or data-flow change.
